<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Curtis's conjecture: in every positive degree, the mod-two stable Hurewicz image of the sphere is spanned by the images of <i>η</i>, <i>ν</i>, <i>σ</i> and the Kervaire-invariant-one classes that exist. Consequently, Eccles's conjecture holds for every sphere <i>S</i><sup><i>n</i></sup> with <i>n</i> &gt; 0.

- **claim-curtis-image-span** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-curtis)): The image of $\bigoplus_{d>0}h_d$ is spanned over $\mathbb F_2$ by the Hurewicz images of the Hopf-invariant-one classes $\eta,\nu,\sigma$, in degrees $1,3,7$, and the Kervaire-invariant-one classes $\theta_j$, in degrees $2^{j+1}-2$, $j\ge1$, whenever they exist. In particular, Curtis’s conjecture is true in all positive degrees.
  - Explanation: The positive stable Hurewicz image is generated by the named Hopf classes and existing Kervaire classes; no additional Kervaire existence is asserted.
- **claim-eccles-positive-spheres** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-eccles-spheres)): For every integer $n>0$, each nonzero mod-2 spherical class in $H_*(QS^n;\mathbb F_2)$ is either $e_n$, or a nonzero class of the form $$\sigma_*^n h_d(\alpha),\qquad (\alpha,d)\in\{(\eta,1),(\nu,3),(\sigma,7)\}.$$ Consequently Eccles’s conjecture holds for every sphere $S^n$, $n>0$: the alternatives are the bottom stably spherical class and Hopf-invariant-one classes.
  - Explanation: Positive-dimensional spheres have only the bottom spherical class and the nonzero suspended Hopf images described in the statement.
- **claim-finite-positive-image** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-image)): The image of $\bigoplus_{d>0}h_d$ is finite, and $$h_d=0\qquad\text{if }d>0\text{ and } d\notin\{1,2,3,6,7,14,30,62,126\}.$$ In particular, the positive stable mod-2 Hurewicz image vanishes above degree $126$.
  - Explanation: The positive image is finite and has the stated degree support. The support is an upper bound rather than an assertion of nonvanishing in every permitted degree.
- **claim-structured-free-ring** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-free-ring)): There is a natural equivalence of augmented commutative ring spectra $$\Sigma^\infty_+ C(X)\simeq\mathbb P(\Sigma^\infty X).$$ The reduced suspension spectrum of $C(X)$, based at its unit, corresponds to the sum of the positive weights. The adjoint of projection to weight one is the canonical map $$C(X)\longrightarrow QX.$$ This map is group completion and is an equivalence when $X$ is connected. In the connected case, the weight-one projection is consequently the stable counit $\Sigma^\infty QX\to\Sigma^\infty X$.
  - Explanation: The reduced free monoid has a natural augmented ring-spectrum model whose positive summands are weights; its weight-one adjoint is group completion.
- **claim-weight-preserving-suspension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weight-suspension)): Under the splitting of Proposition <a href="#prop:free-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:free-ring">4</a>, the stable adjoint suspension on reduced pieces is weight preserving. At weight $r$ it is the natural map $$\label{eq:weight-suspension} \Sigma D_rT\longrightarrow D_r(\Sigma T), \qquad T=\Sigma^\infty X,$$ formed by using the same suspension coordinate in all $r$ factors. For $T=\Sigma^nS$, this is the Thom spectrum map induced by the inclusion of the diagonal trivial line into the permutation representation.* *Under group completion the map is the ordinary homology suspension $\sigma_*:H_*QX\to H_{*+1}Q\Sigma X$. It commutes with the upper-index Dyer–Lashof operations on reduced classes and kills products of two positive-dimensional classes. If $c$ is the class of a component point and $z$ has positive degree, then $$\sigma_*(c*z)=\sigma_*z.$$
  - Explanation: Suspension preserves extended-power weights, commutes with the indicated operations, kills positive products, and ignores component translation of a positive class.
- **claim-suspension-linear-diagonal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-diagonal)): For a suspension $X$, the space diagonal on $C(X)$ corresponds under Proposition <a href="#prop:free-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:free-ring">4</a> to the linear coproduct on $\mathbb P(\Sigma^\infty X)$. Its component from weight $r$ to weights $(i,r-i)$ is the stable transfer for $$\Sigma_i\times\Sigma_{r-i}\subset\Sigma_r.$$
  - Explanation: For a suspension, the reduced diagonal contributes no nonlinear term, so the space coproduct is linear and its weight components are transfers.
- **claim-dickson-invariant-ring** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dickson-ring)): The coefficients $d_0,\ldots,d_{k-1}$ in <a href="#eq:dickson-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dickson-polynomial">[eq:dickson-polynomial]</a> are algebraically independent, have degrees $2^k-2^j$, and generate $P_k^{\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_k(\mathbb F_2)}$.
  - Explanation: The root-polynomial coefficients freely generate the binary general-linear invariant ring with the specified degrees.
- **claim-primitive-weight-modules** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-weight-modules)): The only nonzero positive-weight transfer quotients are at weights $r=2^k$, $k\geq0$. As Steenrod modules they are $$\label{eq:weight-modules} M_0(n)=\Sigma^n\mathbb F_2,\qquad M_k(n)=\Sigma^n d_0^nD_k\quad(k\geq1),\qquad n\geq0.$$ For $n>0$ these give the weight decomposition of $QB_n$. The action on $d_0^nD_k$ is its ordinary action as an ideal in $P_k$.* *The dual of <a href="#eq:weight-suspension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-suspension">[eq:weight-suspension]</a> on these quotients is, after the common grading suspension, the inclusion $$\label{eq:module-suspension} d_0^{n+1}D_k\hookrightarrow d_0^nD_k \qquad(k\geq1).$$ In weight one it is the identity with the corresponding shift.
  - Explanation: Transfer quotients occur only at power-of-two weights and identify with shifted Dickson ideals. Their suspension dual is ideal inclusion.
- **claim-level-zero-leading-weight** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-zero-weights)): There is an equivalence of augmented commutative ring spectra $$\label{eq:zero-localization} R:=\Sigma^\infty_+QS^0 \simeq\mathbb P(S\{u\})[(1+u)^{-1}],\qquad t=1+u,$$ and compatible augmented commutative ring maps $$\label{eq:weight-projections} R\longrightarrow\mathbb P_{\leq N}(S\{u\}),\qquad N\geq0.$$ The induced homology maps jointly detect all classes. Consequently a nonzero positive-dimensional class has a well-defined smallest nonzero weight, called its *leading weight*. Multiplication by a power of $t$ does not change its leading component.* *Let $z\in H_dQ_0S^0$, $d>0$, be primitive and annihilated by positive Steenrod operations. Its leading component $z_r$ is primitive and $A$-annihilated for the linear coproduct calculation of Lemma <a href="#lem:weight-modules" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weight-modules">9</a> at $n=0$. In particular $r=2^k$ for some $k\geq1$. The weight-$r$ component of $\sigma_*z$ is the image of $z_r$ under <a href="#eq:weight-suspension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-suspension">[eq:weight-suspension]</a>; its functional on $M_k(1)$ is the restriction, shifted by one, of the functional on $D_k$ defined by $z_r$. If $d$ is odd, this component is nonzero.
  - Explanation: Localization admits compatible finite weight projections that detect homology. A primitive annihilated class has a primitive annihilated leading component, and an odd-degree leading component survives suspension.
- **claim-suspension-kernel-decomposables** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-suspension-kernel)): For $n\geq1$, a positive-dimensional class in the kernel of $$\sigma_*:H_*QS^n\longrightarrow H_{*+1}QS^{n+1}$$ is decomposable. The same assertion holds for $\sigma_*:H_*Q_0S^0\to H_{*+1}QS^1$. In each case the kernel in positive dimensions is precisely the subspace of decomposables.
  - Explanation: In positive dimensions, the kernel of homology suspension is exactly the decomposable subspace at both positive levels and the base component of level zero.
- **claim-primitive-square-roots** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-primitive-roots)): Let $H$ be a connected graded commutative Hopf algebra over $\mathbb F_2$ which is polynomial as an algebra on positive-degree generators. Every decomposable primitive of $H$ is a square, and its unique square root is primitive. If $H$ also has a Steenrod action satisfying the homology Cartan formula, an $A$-annihilated square has an $A$-annihilated root.* *These statements apply to $H_*Q_0S^0$ and to $H_*QS^n$ for $n\geq1$.
  - Explanation: Polynomial Hopf algebra structure forces decomposable primitives to have unique primitive square roots. Cartan and injective Frobenius preserve annihilation when taking roots.
- **claim-mapping-cone-square-detector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cone-square)): Let $T$ be a connected based space and $m\ge2$. Suppose that $g:S^{2m-1}\to T$ has zero mod-2 Hurewicz image and that its adjoint $g^\flat:S^{2m-2}\to\Omega T$ has Hurewicz image $x^2$, where $x\in H_{m-1}(\Omega_0T;\mathbb F_2)$ and the square uses the loop product. If $u\in H^m(T;\mathbb F_2)$ satisfies $u^2=0$, then it has a unique extension $\widetilde u\in H^m(C_g;\mathbb F_2)$, and $$\label{eq:cone-square} \widetilde u^2=\langle u,\sigma_*x\rangle a,$$ where $a\in H^{2m}(C_g;\mathbb F_2)$ is the image of the top relative class. This relative class is nonzero.
  - Explanation: For an actual sphere attachment with square loop-adjoint image, the square of an extended cohomology class is determined by its pairing with the suspended root.
- **claim-cone-indecomposable-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-cone-extension)): Under these assumptions, the cohomology extension of the cone induces an extension of graded $A$-modules $$\label{eq:indecomposable-cone-extension} 0\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2\{a\}\longrightarrow\mathcal E \longrightarrow Q B\longrightarrow0,$$ where $\|a\|=2m$. On any lift of a degree-$m$ class $v\in Q B$, the operation $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m$ has value $\lambda(v)a$.
  - Explanation: Removing products of distinct lifted exterior generators preserves the relative top class and yields the required Steenrod-module extension.
- **claim-cone-tensor-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-tensor-detection)): If $\lambda(v)=1$ for a degree-$m$ monomial $v$ in a summand $M_k(n)$ of $Q B$, then $$\label{eq:tensor-detection} \mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v\ne0\quad\text{in }I\otimes_A M_k(n).$$ The same conclusion holds for the bottom summand $M_0(n)=\Sigma^n\mathbb F_2$ when its degree is $m$.
  - Explanation: A monomial detected by the cone functional determines a nonzero balanced tensor, including the bottom summand when its degree matches.
- **claim-no-spherical-fourth-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-no-fourth-powers)): There is no positive-dimensional stable sphere class whose nonzero mod-$2$ Hurewicz image is a fourth or higher iterated Pontryagin square. Equivalently, if $f:S^d\to Q_0S^0$, $d>0$, and $$h(f)=z^{*2^a},\qquad a\geq2,$$ then $h(f)=0$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero positive stable spherical image cannot be a fourth or higher iterated Pontryagin square.
- **claim-weight-projection-product-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weight-product)): For points $x,y\in QS^0$, there is a homotopy $$\label{eq:weight-product} j_i(x+y)\simeq j_i(x)+j_i(y) +\sum_{0<t<i}j_t(x)\circ j_{i-t}(y),$$ where addition in the target is its additive infinite loop structure. For positive primitives $A_1,A_2\in H_*Q_0S^0$, this implies $$\label{eq:primitive-weight-product} J_i(A_1*A_2) =(J_iA_1)*(J_iA_2) +\sum_{0<t<i}(J_tA_1)\circ(J_{i-t}A_2).$$ Each $J_i$ preserves primitivity and $A$-annihilation. In particular, $$\label{eq:weight-square} J_i(A^{*2})=(J_iA)^{*2} +\begin{cases}(J_{i/2}A)^{\circ2},&i\text{ even},\\0,&i\text{ odd}. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: Adjoint weight projections preserve primitivity and annihilation but have block-pairing correction terms for additive products and squares.
- **claim-spectrum-pairing-operations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pairing)): Let $E_1\wedge E_2\to E_3$ be a pairing of spectra. If $u\in H_a\Omega^\infty E_1$ is homogeneous and $y\in H_b\Omega^\infty E_2$, where $b>0$, is $A$-annihilated, then, for $i\geq a$, $$\label{eq:pairing-rule} (Q^i u)\circ y= \begin{cases} Q^i(u\circ y),&i\geq a+b,\\ 0,&a\leq i<a+b. \end{cases}$$ If, in addition, $y$ is primitive and belongs to the basepoint component, then $(u_1*u_2)\circ y=0$ for positive-dimensional $u_1,u_2$. Translating a positive-dimensional first factor by a component point does not change its pairing with $y$.
  - Explanation: Coherent spectrum pairings impose an operation-degree threshold and annihilate decomposable first inputs paired with a positive primitive second input.
- **claim-triple-block-nilpotence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-block-nilpotence)): Suppose $B\in H_i(Q_0X_v)$, $i>0$, is primitive and $A$-annihilated. If $\varepsilon_{v*}B=0$, then $$B^{\circ3}=0.$$
  - Explanation: A positive primitive annihilated class with zero stable evaluation has vanishing triple block product.
- **claim-lifted-functional-obstruction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-algebra-obstruction)): Let $k\geq1$, $n=e+1$ with $e\geq0$, and $m\geq2$. Let $v$ be a degree-$m$ monomial of $M_k(n)$. Suppose that its underlying monomial is detected by an annihilated functional on $M_k(e)$ in degree $m-1$; equivalently, $v$ is detected by the shifted restriction of that functional along the ideal inclusion. If $$\label{eq:obstruction-tensor} \mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v\neq0 \qquad\text{in } I\otimes_AM_k(n),$$ then $m$ is a power of two and $n=k=1$.* *Without the functional hypothesis, the tensor in <a href="#eq:obstruction-tensor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:obstruction-tensor">[eq:obstruction-tensor]</a> is still zero whenever $n>0$ and $m\geq3$ is odd.
  - Explanation: A nonzero tensor together with detection inherited from the preceding ideal forces the exceptional rank and level and a power-of-two degree. Odd-degree vanishing does not require that functional.
- **claim-milnor-quotient-halving** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-algebra-milnor-quotient)): There is a quotient homomorphism $$V:A\longrightarrow A,\qquad V(\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{2a})=\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^a,\qquad V(\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{2a+1})=0,$$ where the target is regarded as having doubled degrees. Its kernel is $$K=I_EA=AI_E.$$ Consequently $H_0(E,M)=M/I_EM$ is naturally a module over $A/K$, for every $A$-module $M$.
  - Explanation: The quotient dual to Frobenius halves even Steenrod indices and kills odd ones. Its kernel is generated on either side by positive Milnor primitives.
- **claim-milnor-action-and-coinvariants** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-milnor-coinvariants)): The first $k$ Milnor primitives act on $D_k$ by $$\label{eq:milnor-action} q_i=d_0\frac{\partial}{\partial d_{i+1}}\quad(0\leq i<k-1), \qquad q_{k-1}=d_0\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}d_j\frac{\partial}{\partial d_j}.$$ Every later primitive is a linear combination of these with coefficients that are squares in $D_k$. All ideals $d_0^pD_k$, $p\geq0$, are $A$-submodules.* *The quotient $H_0(E,d_0^pD_k)$ has a monomial basis consisting of all monomials of $d_0$-exponent $p$, together with $$\label{eq:coinvariants} d_0^c\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}d_j^{b_j},\qquad c>p,\quad c\equiv k\pmod2,\quad b_j\text{ odd for every }j\geq1.$$
  - Explanation: Explicit Milnor derivations determine the surviving monomials: all floor monomials survive, while above-floor survivors obey the stated exponent parity conditions.
- **claim-cartier-above-floor-identification** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cartier)): The above-floor part of $H_0(E,d_0^pD_k)$ is an $A/K$-submodule. After identifying $A/K$ with the Steenrod algebra by halving degrees, the map $$\label{eq:cartier} C:\quad d_0^c\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}d_j^{b_j} \longmapsto d_0^{(c+k-2)/2}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}d_j^{(b_j-1)/2}$$ identifies it with an ideal in $D_k$. More precisely, if $c_0$ is the least integer greater than $p$ with $c_0\equiv k\pmod2$, the target ideal is $d_0^{(c_0+k-2)/2}D_k$. Internal polynomial degrees transform by $d\mapsto(d-k)/2$.
  - Explanation: Cartier extraction identifies the above-floor coinvariants with a Dickson ideal, intertwining the halved Steenrod action and preserving the exact target exponent floor.
- **claim-functional-halving-exponent-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-algebra-functional-halving)): Let $n=e+1>0$, let $m$ be even, and let $v$ be a degree-$m$ monomial in $M_k(n)$ with $d_0$-exponent $c$. Suppose that its underlying monomial is detected by an annihilated functional on $M_k(e)$ in degree $m-1$. If $b=2^{\nu_2(m)}$, then $$\label{eq:halving-bounds} n\equiv k\pmod b,\qquad c\geq n+b-2.$$ If $m$ is a power of two, this functional hypothesis alone forces $n=k=1$.
  - Explanation: Repeated halving of an inherited annihilated functional gives a congruence and an exponent lower bound. For a power-of-two degree the functional alone forces the exceptional level and rank.
- **claim-even-cycle-tor-injection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tor-injection)): Let $n\geq0$ and $n\equiv k\pmod2$. On cycles of even total degree, the projection $$\label{eq:tor-injection} I\otimes_A M_k(n)\longrightarrow I_{A/K}\otimes_{A/K}H_0(E,M_k(n))$$ is injective.
  - Explanation: Under the parity condition, passing to Milnor coinvariants cannot kill an even-total-degree tensor cycle.
- **claim-odd-degree-tensor-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-algebra-odd-tensor)): If $n>0$, $m\geq3$ is odd, and $v\in M_k(n)$ is homogeneous of degree $m$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v=0$ in $I\otimes_AM_k(n)$.
  - Explanation: The odd-degree tensor vanishes by an Adem relation, instability, and a further balanced first-square relation in the remaining case.
- **claim-dickson-total-and-near-top-square** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-algebra-near-top-square)): The total Steenrod square on a Dickson generator is $$\label{eq:algebra-total-dickson-square} \mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(d_j)=d_j^2+ \left(\sum_{i=0}^jd_i\right) \left(\sum_{a=j+1}^kd_a\right).$$ In particular the operation one below the top on $d_j$ is $d_0d_{j+1}$. For a positive-degree monomial $v=\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}d_j^{b_j}$ of internal degree $d$, $$\label{eq:algebra-one-below-top} \mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{d-1}v =v^2\sum_{j:\ b_j\ \text{odd}}\frac{d_0d_{j+1}}{d_j^2}.$$ Every displayed summand is a polynomial.
  - Explanation: The total square of a Dickson generator determines an explicit polynomial formula for the operation immediately below the top on any positive-degree monomial.
- **claim-even-nonpower-tensor-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing)): Let $m$ be even and not a power of two, let $b=2^{\nu_2(m)}$, and let $v\in M_k(n)$ be a degree-$m$ monomial with $n>0$ and $d_0$-exponent $c$. If <a href="#eq:halving-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:halving-bounds">[eq:halving-bounds]</a> holds, then $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v=0$ in $I\otimes_AM_k(n)$.
  - Explanation: The exponent and congruence bounds permit halving an even-degree tensor until its non-power-of-two degree forces vanishing.
- **claim-terminal-square-root** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-last-root)): For some $m\ge2$, $$\label{eq:last-square} z_e=w^2,\qquad \|w\|=m-1,\qquad d+e=2m-2,$$ where $w$ is primitive and $A$-annihilated, and $\sigma_*w\ne0$. If $e\ge1$, then $\|w\|$ is odd.
  - Explanation: The last nonzero suspended spherical image is a square with primitive annihilated root whose suspension survives. At a positive terminal level the root has odd degree.
- **claim-positive-terminal-hopf-detection** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-hopf-case)): If $e\ge1$, then $\alpha$ has nonzero mod-2 stable Hopf invariant and $d\in\{1,3,7\}$.
  - Explanation: At a positive terminal level the algebra obstruction removes every nonbottom weight, so stable cone detection gives a Hopf-invariant-one stem.
- **claim-zero-terminal-kervaire-detection** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-kervaire-case)): If $e=0$, then for some $j\ge1$, $$d=2^{j+1}-2,$$ and $\alpha$ has Kervaire invariant one.
  - Explanation: At terminal level zero the root has leading weight two and the original image has leading weight four. The Adams filtration bound and elementwise detection identify the given class as Kervaire invariant one.

As Steenrod modules they are $$\\label{eq:weight-modules}\n M_0(n)=\\Sigma^n\\mathbb F_2,\\qquad\n M_k(n)=\\Sigma^n d_0^nD_k\\quad(k\\geq1),\\qquad n\\geq0.$$ For $n>0$ these give the weight decomposition of $QB_n$. The action on $d_0^nD_k$ is its ordinary action as an ideal in $P_k$.*\n\n\n*The dual of <a href=\"#eq:weight-suspension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weight-suspension\">[eq:weight-suspension]</a> on these quotients is, after the common grading suspension, the inclusion $$\\label{eq:module-suspension}\n d_0^{n+1}D_k\\hookrightarrow d_0^nD_k\n \\qquad(k\\geq1).$$ In weight one it is the identity with the corresponding shift.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-zero-weights",
        "exact_quote": "There is an equivalence of augmented commutative ring spectra $$\\label{eq:zero-localization}\n R:=\\Sigma^\\infty_+QS^0\n   \\simeq\\mathbb P(S\\{u\\})[(1+u)^{-1}],\\qquad t=1+u,$$ and compatible augmented commutative ring maps $$\\label{eq:weight-projections}\n R\\longrightarrow\\mathbb P_{\\leq N}(S\\{u\\}),\\qquad N\\geq0.$$ The induced homology maps jointly detect all classes. Consequently a nonzero positive-dimensional class has a well-defined smallest nonzero weight, called its *leading weight*. Multiplication by a power of $t$ does not change its leading component.*\n\n*Let $z\\in H_dQ_0S^0$, $d>0$, be primitive and annihilated by positive Steenrod operations. Its leading component $z_r$ is primitive and $A$-annihilated for the linear coproduct calculation of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:weight-modules\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:weight-modules\">9</a> at $n=0$. In particular $r=2^k$ for some $k\\geq1$. The weight-$r$ component of $\\sigma_*z$ is the image of $z_r$ under <a href=\"#eq:weight-suspension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weight-suspension\">[eq:weight-suspension]</a>; its functional on $M_k(1)$ is the restriction, shifted by one, of the functional on $D_k$ defined by $z_r$. If $d$ is odd, this component is nonzero.",
        "source_id": "PM-316.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-primitive-roots",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a connected graded commutative Hopf algebra over $\\mathbb F_2$ which is polynomial as an algebra on positive-degree generators. Every decomposable primitive of $H$ is a square, and its unique square root is primitive. If $H$ also has a Steenrod action satisfying the homology Cartan formula, an $A$-annihilated square has an $A$-annihilated root.*\n\n*These statements apply to $H_*Q_0S^0$ and to $H_*QS^n$ for $n\\geq1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-316.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a connected based space and $m\\ge2$. If $u\\in H^m(T;\\mathbb F_2)$ satisfies $u^2=0$, then it has a unique extension $\\widetilde u\\in H^m(C_g;\\mathbb F_2)$, and $$\\label{eq:cone-square}\n\\widetilde u^2=\\langle u,\\sigma_*x\\rangle a,$$ where $a\\in H^{2m}(C_g;\\mathbb F_2)$ is the image of the top relative class.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weight-product",
        "exact_quote": "For points $x,y\\in QS^0$, there is a homotopy $$\\label{eq:weight-product}\n j_i(x+y)\\simeq j_i(x)+j_i(y)\n       +\\sum_{0<t<i}j_t(x)\\circ j_{i-t}(y),$$ where addition in the target is its additive infinite loop structure. For positive primitives $A_1,A_2\\in H_*Q_0S^0$, this implies $$\\label{eq:primitive-weight-product}\n J_i(A_1*A_2)\n =(J_iA_1)*(J_iA_2)\n   +\\sum_{0<t<i}(J_tA_1)\\circ(J_{i-t}A_2).$$ Each $J_i$ preserves primitivity and $A$-annihilation. In particular, $$\\label{eq:weight-square}\n J_i(A^{*2})=(J_iA)^{*2}\n +\\begin{cases}(J_{i/2}A)^{\\circ2},&i\\text{ even},\\\\0,&i\\text{ odd}.\n \\end{cases}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-316.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-weight-product",
          "exact_quote": "For points $x,y\\in QS^0$, there is a homotopy $$\\label{eq:weight-product}\n j_i(x+y)\\simeq j_i(x)+j_i(y)\n       +\\sum_{0<t<i}j_t(x)\\circ j_{i-t}(y),$$ where addition in the target is its additive infinite loop structure. For positive primitives $A_1,A_2\\in H_*Q_0S^0$, this implies $$\\label{eq:primitive-weight-product}\n J_i(A_1*A_2)\n =(J_iA_1)*(J_iA_2)\n   +\\sum_{0<t<i}(J_tA_1)\\circ(J_{i-t}A_2).$$ Each $J_i$ preserves primitivity and $A$-annihilation. In particular, $$\\label{eq:weight-square}\n J_i(A^{*2})=(J_iA)^{*2}\n +\\begin{cases}(J_{i/2}A)^{\\circ2},&i\\text{ even},\\\\0,&i\\text{ odd}.\n \\end{cases}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-316.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For points $x,y\\in QS^0$, there is a homotopy $$\\label{eq:weight-product}\n j_i(x+y)\\simeq j_i(x)+j_i(y)\n       +\\sum_{0<t<i}j_t(x)\\circ j_{i-t}(y),$$ where addition in the target is its additive infinite loop structure. For positive primitives $A_1,A_2\\in H_*Q_0S^0$, this implies $$\\label{eq:primitive-weight-product}\n J_i(A_1*A_2)\n =(J_iA_1)*(J_iA_2)\n   +\\sum_{0<t<i}(J_tA_1)\\circ(J_{i-t}A_2).$$ Each $J_i$ preserves primitivity and $A$-annihilation. In particular, $$\\label{eq:weight-square}\n J_i(A^{*2})=(J_iA)^{*2}\n +\\begin{cases}(J_{i/2}A)^{\\circ2},&i\\text{ even},\\\\0,&i\\text{ odd}.\n \\end{cases}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-algebra-obstruction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k\\geq1$, $n=e+1$ with $e\\geq0$, and $m\\geq2$. Let $v$ be a degree-$m$ monomial of $M_k(n)$. Suppose that its underlying monomial is detected by an annihilated functional on $M_k(e)$ in degree $m-1$; equivalently, $v$ is detected by the shifted restriction of that functional along the ideal inclusion. If $$\\label{eq:obstruction-tensor}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Sq}}^m\\otimes v\\neq0\n       \\qquad\\text{in } I\\otimes_AM_k(n),$$ then $m$ is a power of two and $n=k=1$.*\n\n*Without the functional hypothesis, the tensor in <a href=\"#eq:obstruction-tensor\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:obstruction-tensor\">[eq:obstruction-tensor]</a> is still zero whenever $n>0$ and $m\\geq3$ is odd.",
        "source_id": "PM-316.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-milnor-coinvariants",
        "exact_quote": "The first $k$ Milnor primitives act on $D_k$ by $$\\label{eq:milnor-action}\n q_i=d_0\\frac{\\partial}{\\partial d_{i+1}}\\quad(0\\leq i<k-1),\n \\qquad\n q_{k-1}=d_0\\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}d_j\\frac{\\partial}{\\partial d_j}.$$ Every later primitive is a linear combination of these with coefficients that are squares in $D_k$. All ideals $d_0^pD_k$, $p\\geq0$, are $A$-submodules.*\n\n\n*The quotient $H_0(E,d_0^pD_k)$ has a monomial basis consisting of all monomials of $d_0$-exponent $p$, together with $$\\label{eq:coinvariants}\n d_0^c\\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}d_j^{b_j},\\qquad\n c>p,\\quad c\\equiv k\\pmod2,\\quad b_j\\text{ odd for every }j\\geq1.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-316.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cartier",
        "exact_quote": "The above-floor part of $H_0(E,d_0^pD_k)$ is an $A/K$-submodule. After identifying $A/K$ with the Steenrod algebra by halving degrees, the map $$\\label{eq:cartier}\n C:\\quad d_0^c\\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}d_j^{b_j}\n \\longmapsto\n d_0^{(c+k-2)/2}\\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}d_j^{(b_j-1)/2}$$ identifies it with an ideal in $D_k$. More precisely, if $c_0$ is the least integer greater than $p$ with $c_0\\equiv k\\pmod2$, the target ideal is $d_0^{(c_0+k-2)/2}D_k$. Internal polynomial degrees transform by $d\\mapsto(d-k)/2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-316.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Infinite loop sphere space** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For $n\ge0$, let $QS^n=\operatorname*{colim}_r\Omega^rS^{n+r}$, and let $Q_0S^0$ be its basepoint component.
- **Stable mod-2 Hurewicz homomorphism** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For $d>0$, the identification $\pi_d(Q_0S^0)=\pi_d^S$ gives the mod-2 Hurewicz homomorphism $$h_d:\pi_d^S\longrightarrow H_d(Q_0S^0;\mathbb F_2).$$
- **Steenrod algebra and augmentation ideal** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Let $A$ be the mod-2 Steenrod algebra and $I=A^{>0}$ its augmentation ideal.
- **Steenrod-annihilated homology class** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A homology class is $A$-annihilated if every $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}_*^i$, $i>0$, kills it.
- **Cohomology indecomposables** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a positive graded cohomology algebra $B$, write $Q B=B/B^2$ for its indecomposables.
- **Extended power and weight** ([source](#sec:weights)): For a spectrum $T$, put $$D_rT=(T^{\wedge r})_{h\Sigma_r},\qquad \mathbb P(T)=\bigvee_{r\geq0}D_rT,\qquad D_0T=S.$$ Thus $\mathbb P(T)$ is the free commutative ring spectrum on $T$, and its $r$th summand has *weight* $r$.
- **Reduced free commutative monoid** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Let $C(X)$ be the reduced free $E_\infty$ monoid on a based space $X$: the basepoint of $X$ is identified with the monoid unit.
- **Primitive transfer quotient** ([source](#pm-h-8)): At weight $r>0$, let $P_r(n)$ denote the subspace of $H_*D_r(\Sigma^nS)$ primitive for the linear coproduct: all components with $0<i<r$ vanish.
- **Dickson generators** ([source](#eq:dickson-polynomial)): The Dickson generators are the coefficients of the additive polynomial $$\label{eq:dickson-polynomial} \prod_{a\in\operatorname{span}\{x_1,\ldots,x_k\}}(U+a) =\sum_{j=0}^k d_jU^{2^j}.$$
- **Primitive-weight Steenrod modules** ([source](#stmt-lem-weight-modules)): As Steenrod modules they are $$\label{eq:weight-modules} M_0(n)=\Sigma^n\mathbb F_2,\qquad M_k(n)=\Sigma^n d_0^nD_k\quad(k\geq1),\qquad n\geq0.$$
- **Leading weight** ([source](#stmt-lem-zero-weights)): Consequently a nonzero positive-dimensional class has a well-defined smallest nonzero weight, called its *leading weight*.
- **Annihilated functional** ([source](#eq:algebra-degree-parity)): An annihilated functional on a graded $A$-module $M$ is a homogeneous linear functional $\lambda$ such that $\lambda(IM)=0$.
- **Milnor primitive exterior algebra** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Let $E\subset A$ be the exterior sub-Hopf algebra on the Milnor primitives $q_i$, indexed by $\|q_i\|=2^{i+1}-1$ for $i\geq0$.
- **Above-floor coinvariants** ([source](#pm-h-15)): We call the span of the monomials in <a href="#eq:coinvariants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coinvariants">[eq:coinvariants]</a> the *above-floor part* of the coinvariants.
- **Tensor cycles** ([source](#pm-h-17)): We refer to elements of this kernel as *cycles*.

          "exact_quote": "As Steenrod modules they are $$\\label{eq:weight-modules}\n M_0(n)=\\Sigma^n\\mathbb F_2,\\qquad\n M_k(n)=\\Sigma^n d_0^nD_k\\quad(k\\geq1),\\qquad n\\geq0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-316.1"
        }
      ],

      "interpretation": "Primitive weights are represented dually by shifted Dickson ideals, with a separate bottom summand.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-free-ring-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-free-ring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-free-ring): Apply the reduced free universal property and preservation of its pushout by suspension. -> Structured augmented ring-spectrum splitting and weight-one counit.
- **step-weight-suspension** [claim](#stmt-lem-weight-suspension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weight-suspension): Apply based mapping-space naturality to the suspension-coordinate family. -> Weight-preserving suspension and its product rules.
- **step-linear-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-lem-linear-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-linear-diagonal): Null the reduced diagonal of a suspension and expand extended powers of a linear sum. -> Linear coproduct with block-transfer components.
- **step-dickson-ring** [claim](#stmt-lem-dickson-ring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dickson-ring): Use the additive root polynomial, integral extension, and splitting-field invariants. -> Polynomial presentation of the Dickson invariant ring.
- **step-weight-modules** [claim](#stmt-lem-weight-modules) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weight-modules): Compare primitive word degrees with restriction to a regular elementary abelian subgroup and Thom pullback. -> Primitive-weight modules and suspension as ideal inclusion.
- **step-zero-leading-weight** [claim](#stmt-lem-zero-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-zero-weights): Localize at the particle, extend finite truncations, and compare the leading associated coproduct. -> Jointly detecting finite projections and the level-zero leading-weight suspension rule.
- **step-suspension-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-suspension-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-suspension-kernel): Track distinct polynomial generators under suspension, retaining squares in the target homology. -> The positive-dimensional suspension kernel is precisely decomposables.
- **step-primitive-roots** [claim](#stmt-lem-primitive-roots) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-primitive-roots): Use the coproduct derivation to force even exponents, then apply injective Frobenius and Cartan. -> Unique primitive annihilated roots of decomposable primitives.
- **step-cone-square** [claim](#stmt-lem-cone-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cone-square): Lift the class to the fibre of its square operation and detect the bottom loop square by the cobar filtration. -> Cone-square coefficient equals the suspended-root pairing.
- **step-cone-extension** [claim](#stmt-prop-cone-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cone-extension): Choose lifted exterior generators and quotient only products of distinct generators. -> An indecomposable Steenrod-module extension retaining the relative class.
- **step-tensor-detection** [claim](#stmt-cor-tensor-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-tensor-detection): Evaluate the section defect on the balanced tensor product. -> Nonzero square-operation tensor.
- **step-projection-products** [claim](#stmt-lem-weight-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weight-product): Expand multiplication in a single truncated augmented ring and distribute primitive diagonals. -> Projection product formula with block-square correction.
- **step-spectrum-pairing-rule** [claim](#stmt-lem-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pairing): Compute the extended-square diagonal by transfer and external-square evaluations. -> Operation threshold, decomposable vanishing, and translation invariance.
- **step-block-nilpotence** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-nilpotence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-nilpotence): Remove length-zero generators by stable evaluation and apply the pairing threshold to remaining words. -> Triple block nilpotence.
- **step-exclude-fourth-powers** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-fourth-powers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-nilpotence-2): Detect a leading square by even characteristic classes, remove the block correction, and contradict the cone square using an integral reduction. -> No nonzero positive stable spherical fourth or higher iterated square.
- **step-milnor-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-algebra-milnor-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-algebra-milnor-quotient): Dualize Frobenius in the polynomial Steenrod dual and identify its annihilator by the Milnor coaction. -> Degree-halving quotient with the stated two-sided kernel.
- **step-milnor-coinvariants** [claim](#stmt-lem-milnor-coinvariants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-milnor-coinvariants): Differentiate the root polynomial, express later derivations with square coefficients, and classify monomial hits. -> Explicit action and coinvariant basis with its exponent floor.
- **step-cartier-identification** [claim](#stmt-lem-cartier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cartier): Use odd-exponent extraction, substitution naturality, and the Dickson Jacobian. -> Above-floor submodule identified with its exact target ideal.
- **step-functional-exponent-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-algebra-functional-halving) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-algebra-functional-halving): Restrict the inherited functional above the preceding floor and iterate Cartier halving while tracking shifts. -> Congruence and exponent bound, with exceptional parameters in power-of-two degree.
- **step-even-tor-injection** [claim](#stmt-lem-tor-injection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tor-injection): Reduce the kernel to exterior cycles, fill them after localization, and discard filling terms below the allowed floor. -> Injectivity on even-total-degree cycles.
- **step-odd-tensor-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-lem-algebra-odd-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-algebra-odd-tensor): Balance an Adem decomposition and use instability or an explicit first-square preimage. -> Vanishing of the odd-degree square tensor.
- **step-near-top-squares** [claim](#stmt-lem-algebra-near-top-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-algebra-near-top-square): Apply the total square to the additive root polynomial and compare coefficients, then use Cartan. -> Total and near-top square formulas.
- **step-even-tensor-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing): Induct by coinvariant halving with Tor injectivity and handle the final floor using the near-top square witness. -> Vanishing in even degrees that are not powers of two.
- **step-algebra-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-algebra-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing-2): Combine odd vanishing, even non-power vanishing, and the power-of-two functional bound. -> Exceptional rank and level with a power-of-two degree.
- **step-terminal-root** [claim](#stmt-lem-last-root) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-last-root): Apply the suspension-kernel and root lemmas, then use preceding-level parity or fourth-power exclusion. -> Primitive annihilated root with nonzero suspension and positive-level odd parity.
- **step-hopf-case** [claim](#stmt-prop-hopf-case) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hopf-case): Exclude nonbottom weights by the obstruction and pull cone detection to the stable two-cell cofiber. -> Hopf detection in the stated positive stems.
- **step-kervaire-case** [claim](#stmt-prop-kervaire-case) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-kervaire-case): Force the leading weight by the obstruction, bound Adams filtration with derived ideal powers, and apply elementwise Kervaire detection. -> Kervaire invariant one for the given class in the stated stem.
- **step-curtis-span** [claim](#stmt-thm-curtis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-kervaire-case-2): Use additive detecting invariants to subtract an existing representative and force its difference into the Hurewicz kernel. -> The asserted positive stable Hurewicz span.
- **step-eccles-spheres** [claim](#stmt-cor-eccles-spheres) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-eccles-spheres): Use adjunction and suspension naturality, kill Kervaire squares, and retain the bottom unit by connectivity. -> Eccles classification for positive-dimensional spheres.
- **step-finite-image-support** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-image): Combine the Curtis span with the cited Kervaire nonexistence bound. -> Finite positive image and the stated support ceiling.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-positive-stable-image**: The positive stable Hurewicz image is spanned by the Hopf-invariant-one images and the Kervaire-invariant-one images that exist.
- **invariant-terminal-root**: The last nonzero homology suspension is a square with a primitive, Steenrod-annihilated root that survives homology suspension.
- **invariant-lifted-functional-obstruction**: A nonzero detecting tensor on a nonbottom weight, together with an annihilated detecting functional lifted from the preceding level, forces a power-of-two degree and the exceptional lowest level and rank.
- **invariant-block-nilpotence**: A positive-dimensional primitive, Steenrod-annihilated class with zero stable homology evaluation has vanishing triple block power.
- **invariant-finite-support**: The positive stable mod-2 Hurewicz image is finite and vanishes above degree 126; the displayed support is an upper bound.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-necessary-conditions-as-realization**: Certify that a homology class is spherical solely because it is primitive and Steenrod-annihilated. -> Algebraic necessary conditions do not supply a sphere representative or the mapping-cone extension used in the proof.
- **anti-general-eccles-extension**: Apply the sphere corollary to arbitrary connected spaces. -> The proved realization alternatives are restricted to positive-dimensional spheres.
- **anti-support-as-existence**: Treat every listed possible support degree as a certified nonzero image or infer the exact dimension of the image. -> An upper bound on support does not provide nonvanishing or independence certificates.
- **anti-drop-functional-lift**: Infer the exceptional level and rank from tensor nonvanishing alone. -> The even-degree obstruction uses the functional from the preceding level to control exponent floors during halving.
- **anti-weight-projection-additivity**: Push Pontryagin squares through an individual adjoint weight projection without correction. -> The adjoint projections can introduce block-square terms.
- **anti-level-zero-linear-diagonal**: Identify the auxiliary linear coproduct with the actual space diagonal at level zero. -> The actual diagonal has an additional quadratic term; the linear coproduct describes the associated leading-weight calculation.
- **anti-filtration-equals-weight**: Read an exact Adams filtration directly from the leading weight. -> The proof supplies a bound through ideal powers and truncated weight projections, rather than an equality between filtrations.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result classifies Hurewicz images of actual stable sphere maps through mapping-cone detection and Adams filtration. A finite image does not supply a finite constructive decision procedure for realization. The source states: "A spherical class must be primitive and annihilated by positive Steenrod operations, but these necessary conditions alone do not establish its realization." It also states: "The theorem does not posit existence in any additional degree." Finite checks of polynomial identities would cover auxiliary algebra, without deciding the sphere realization and detection claims.

For positive primitives $A_1,A_2\\in H_*Q_0S^0$, this implies $$\\label{eq:primitive-weight-product}\n J_i(A_1*A_2)\n =(J_iA_1)*(J_iA_2)\n   +\\sum_{0<t<i}(J_tA_1)\\circ(J_{i-t}A_2).$$ Each $J_i$ preserves primitivity and $A$-annihilation. In particular, $$\\label{eq:weight-square}\n J_i(A^{*2})=(J_iA)^{*2}\n +\\begin{cases}(J_{i/2}A)^{\\circ2},&i\\text{ even},\\\\0,&i\\text{ odd}.\n \\end{cases}$$",
            "source_object": "stmt-lem-weight-product"
          }
        ],

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The structured free-algebra equivalence concerns spectra and homotopy coherent maps; it supplies no application interface or executable software contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sphere realization problem classifies spherical homology classes without defining simulation states, update rules, or game dynamics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weight projections are maps of augmented commutative ring spectra. Their joint detection property does not provide an executable tool for accepting arbitrary topological inputs. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The explicit Cartier monomial transformation supports a finite symbolic reduction of an above-floor Milnor-coinvariant representative to a monomial in the specified Dickson ideal. This construction implements the algebraic transformation rather than deciding spherical realization. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polynomial presentation and monomial basis describe algebraic objects, but the paper provides no storage representation, indexing operations, or data-structure performance contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The pairing calculation concerns spectrum maps and homology operations; it contains no hardware architecture, scheduling construction, or accelerator resource model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The quotient of the Steenrod algebra is a homological algebra construction, with no adversary model, secret material, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lifted functional is an annihilated linear functional on a Steenrod module, not a learned predictor or an objective fitted from samples. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The algebra obstruction restricts when a balanced tensor can remain nonzero; it defines neither an optimisation objective nor a feasible-set search procedure. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Block nilpotence is a vanishing statement about primitive homology classes under spectrum pairings, without a communication, fault, or distributed execution model. |
| other (Symbolic invariant theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The root-polynomial construction gives a finite exact method for obtaining Dickson generators at a supplied rank. The invariant-ring lemma identifies the resulting coefficients as generators of the binary general linear invariant algebra. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-positive-mod-two-sphere-scope** (formal_scope): The classification concerns the positive stable sphere Hurewicz image with mod-2 coefficients. Blocked use: ['Applying the classification to odd-primary Hurewicz maps.', 'Including the degree-zero component in the asserted positive-degree span.']
- **limit-existing-kervaire-representatives** (hypothesis): Kervaire representatives enter the span only when they exist; the classification does not establish additional existence. Blocked use: ['Constructing a Kervaire representative merely from its formally permitted degree.']
- **limit-eccles-target-spheres** (formal_scope): The Eccles conclusion is restricted to positive-dimensional spheres. Blocked use: ['Extending the stated realization alternatives to arbitrary connected spaces.']

Blocked use: ['Treating every allowed degree as an existence certificate.', 'Counting the listed degrees as the exact dimension of the image.']
- **limit-realization-needs-actual-map** (hypothesis): Primitivity and Steenrod annihilation are necessary conditions, while the realization argument additionally uses an actual sphere map and its cone. Blocked use: ['Certifying a proposed homology class as spherical from algebraic annihilation tests alone.']

- **limit-level-zero-diagonal** (edge_case): At level zero the linear coproduct is auxiliary. Its primitive calculation applies to the leading associated weight rather than directly replacing the actual space diagonal. Blocked use: ['Discarding the nonlinear diagonal contribution before passing to leading weight.']
- **limit-suspension-target-full-homology** (formal_scope): The suspension-kernel argument retains the full homology of the target, including primitive words that are squares. Blocked use: ['Projecting the target to indecomposables before testing injectivity on source generators.']
- **limit-polynomial-hopf-root-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Primitive square-root extraction uses connected graded polynomial Hopf algebra structure over the binary field. Preservation of annihilation additionally requires the homology Cartan formula. Blocked use: ['Applying unique-root or primitive-root conclusions in an arbitrary Hopf algebra.', 'Transferring Steenrod annihilation to a root without the stated action compatibility.']
- **limit-cone-square-preconditions** (hypothesis): The cone-square detector requires a connected target, the stated dimension range, vanishing Hurewicz image of the attachment, a square loop-adjoint image, and a target cohomology class with vanishing square. Blocked use: ['Using the cone-square formula for an arbitrary attachment or an arbitrary target cohomology class.']
- **limit-cone-quotient-retains-top-class** (formal_scope): The cone-module construction removes products of distinct lifted generators rather than all products, so that the relative top class is retained. Blocked use: ['Replacing the constructed cone quotient by ordinary indecomposables when the relative class is itself a square.']
- **limit-adjoint-projection-product-correction** (edge_case): Individual adjoint weight projections have block-pairing corrections to the Pontryagin product formula. Blocked use: ['Pushing a Pontryagin square through an adjoint weight projection as though the projection were additive.']
- **limit-block-nilpotence-preconditions** (hypothesis): Triple block nilpotence requires a positive-dimensional primitive, Steenrod-annihilated class in the stated basepoint component with zero stable homology evaluation. Blocked use: ['Inferring block nilpotence from primitivity alone.', 'Confusing block powers with additive Pontryagin powers.']

Blocked use: ['Applying the above-floor transformation indiscriminately to every coinvariant basis monomial.', 'Dropping the target exponent threshold during repeated halving.']
- **limit-functional-and-tensor-floors-differ** (formal_scope): Functional halving and tensor halving start from adjacent ideals with different exponent floors. Blocked use: ["Using the functional's ideal floor unchanged when halving the tensor."]
- **limit-tor-injection-even-cycles** (hypothesis): The coinvariant projection is asserted to be injective on even-total-degree cycles under the stated parity condition. - **limit-root-not-necessarily-spherical** (formal_scope): The terminal square root need not itself be spherical; the next adjoint and its mapping cone supply the realization information. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Curtis's conjecture: in every positive degree, the mod-two stable Hurewicz image of the sphere is spanned by the images of <i>η</i>, <i>ν</i>, <i>σ</i> and the Kervaire-invariant-one classes that exist. Consequently, Eccles's conjecture holds for every sphere <i>S</i><sup><i>n</i></sup> with <i>n</i> &gt; 0.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

For $n\ge0$, let $QS^n=\operatorname*{colim}_r\Omega^rS^{n+r}$, and let $Q_0S^0$ be its basepoint component. For $d>0$, the identification $\pi_d(Q_0S^0)=\pi_d^S$ gives the mod-2 Hurewicz homomorphism $$h_d:\pi_d^S\longrightarrow H_d(Q_0S^0;\mathbb F_2).$$ It sends a representative $S^d\to Q_0S^0$ to the image of the mod-2 fundamental class. Curtis’s conjecture describes the positive-degree image of these maps (Curtis 1975) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Curtis1975](#ref-Curtis1975)]. The target has an explicit homology algebra, while the stable homotopy groups in the source contain many families whose images are difficult to determine. The image problem asks which of the readily described homology classes are represented by maps from spheres.

Curtis proposed the Hopf–Kervaire description in his study of the Dyer–Lashof and lambda algebras (Curtis 1975) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Curtis1975](#ref-Curtis1975)]. May’s subsequent account records a gap found by Wellington in the proposed proof (May 1977, 484, footnote 3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[May1977](#ref-May1977)]. We prove the following form of the conjecture.

<a id="stmt-thm-curtis"></a>
<div id="thm:curtis" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *The image of $\bigoplus_{d>0}h_d$ is spanned over $\mathbb F_2$ by the Hurewicz images of the Hopf-invariant-one classes $\eta,\nu,\sigma$, in degrees $1,3,7$, and the Kervaire-invariant-one classes $\theta_j$, in degrees $2^{j+1}-2$, $j\ge1$, whenever they exist. In particular, Curtis’s conjecture is true in all positive degrees.*

</div>

Here Kervaire invariant one means that the corresponding framed manifold, under the Pontryagin–Thom identification, has Arf invariant one. The notation $\theta_j$ is used only when such a class exists. The theorem does not posit existence in any additional degree.

Eccles’s conjecture concerns the analogous realization problem for $QX$. In the formulation recorded in (Zare 2009, 14–15) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Zare2009Thesis](#ref-Zare2009Thesis)], for a path-connected CW complex $X$ of finite type, a positive-dimensional sphere map to $QX$ with nonzero mod-2 Hurewicz image has stable adjoint detected either by homology or by a primary cohomology operation in its mapping cone. Theorem <a href="#thm:curtis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:curtis">1</a> gives this conclusion when $X$ is a positive-dimensional sphere.

For $n>0$, write $e_n\in H_n(QS^n;\mathbb F_2)$ for the bottom spherical class, and write $\sigma_*^n$ for $n$-fold homology suspension from $Q_0S^0$.

<a id="stmt-cor-eccles-spheres"></a>
<div id="cor:eccles-spheres" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Eccles’s conjecture for spheres). *For every integer $n>0$, each nonzero mod-2 spherical class in $H_*(QS^n;\mathbb F_2)$ is either $e_n$, or a nonzero class of the form $$\sigma_*^n h_d(\alpha),\qquad
 (\alpha,d)\in\{(\eta,1),(\nu,3),(\sigma,7)\}.$$ Consequently Eccles’s conjecture holds for every sphere $S^n$, $n>0$: the alternatives are the bottom stably spherical class and Hopf-invariant-one classes.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-eccles-spheres"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-eccles-spheres</summary>

*Proof.* For a spherical representative $f:S^{n+d}\to QS^n$ with $d>0$, adjunction gives $f_0:S^d\to Q_0S^0$, and naturality gives $h(f)=\sigma_*^n h_d([f_0])$. By Theorem <a href="#thm:curtis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:curtis">1</a>, the level-zero image is spanned by the Hopf and Kervaire images. The latter are squares killed by the first homology suspension, leaving the stated Hopf possibilities; this is the Curtis-to-Eccles sphere implication recorded in (Zare 2009, chap. 5, pp. 81–82) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Zare2009Thesis](#ref-Zare2009Thesis)]. In degree $n$, $\pi_n(QS^n)=\pi_0^S=\mathbb Z$ gives the bottom image represented by the unit $S^n\to QS^n$; connectivity excludes lower degrees. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-finite-image"></a>
<div id="cor:finite-image" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Finite positive stable Hurewicz image). *The image of $\bigoplus_{d>0}h_d$ is finite, and $$h_d=0\qquad\text{if }d>0\text{ and }
 d\notin\{1,2,3,6,7,14,30,62,126\}.$$ In particular, the positive stable mod-2 Hurewicz image vanishes above degree $126$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-finite-image"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-finite-image</summary>

*Proof.* Hill, Hopkins, and Ravenel prove that $\theta_j$ can exist only for $j\le6$ (Hill, Hopkins, and Ravenel 2016, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HillHopkinsRavenel2016](#ref-HillHopkinsRavenel2016)]. By Theorem <a href="#thm:curtis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:curtis">1</a>, the positive image is therefore spanned by the named images in Hopf degrees $1,3,7$ and Kervaire degrees $2,6,14,30,62,126$. Finitely many generators over $\mathbb F_2$ give a finite image and the stated support. ◻

</details>

Corollary <a href="#cor:eccles-spheres" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:eccles-spheres">2</a> concerns only positive-dimensional spheres, not general spaces. The support in Corollary <a href="#cor:finite-image" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finite-image">3</a> is an upper bound; it does not assert nonvanishing in every listed degree or an exact dimension of the image.

Adams’s Hopf invariant one theorem identifies the Hopf cases (Adams 1960) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Adams1960](#ref-Adams1960)]. Browder’s work relates the framed Kervaire invariant to the classes $h_j^2$ in the mod-2 Adams spectral sequence (Browder 1969) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Browder1969](#ref-Browder1969)]. Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel’s nonexistence theorem leaves dimension $126$ as the last possible Kervaire dimension. In their preprint, Lin, Wang, and Xu prove existence in that dimension (Lin, Wang, and Xu 2025, Theorems 1.1 and 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[LinWangXu2025](#ref-LinWangXu2025)]. This endpoint result is separate from the upper bound used in Corollary <a href="#cor:finite-image" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finite-image">3</a>.

On the homology side, the free infinite-loop-space calculation describes a large algebra generated by Dyer–Lashof operations (Cohen, Lada, and May 1976) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CLM1976](#ref-CLM1976)]. A spherical class must be primitive and annihilated by positive Steenrod operations, but these necessary conditions alone do not establish its realization. The theorem concerns the image of actual stable homotopy classes.

Eccles and Zare give restrictions on spherical squares and their roots, including the exclusion of higher iterated squares (Eccles and Zare 2009, Theorem 3.10 and Lemma 3.12) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[EcclesZare2009](#ref-EcclesZare2009)]. Section <a href="#sec:powers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:powers">4</a> proves the exclusion of fourth powers by spectrum pairings and integral characteristic classes; the conclusion itself is already present in that prior work. The Dickson-algebra approach to spherical classes also appears in Hung and Peterson’s study of the Lannes–Zarati homomorphism and products in the unstable Adams $E_2$-term (Hung and Peterson 1998) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[HungPeterson1998](#ref-HungPeterson1998)]. Hunter’s cohomological construction of the Curtis–Wellington spectral sequence exhibits Dickson-algebra quotients in a width filtration (Hunter 2025, arXiv version 2, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Hunter2025](#ref-Hunter2025)].

The argument here combines classical detection by mapping-cone operations with an obstruction for the specific Steenrod modules dual to primitives. The cone of an *actual* sphere map supplies a nonzero extension. A detecting functional inherited from the preceding suspension level then restricts that extension through the algebraic obstruction proved in Section <a href="#sec:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algebra">5</a>.

The free-algebra model constructed below is compatible with Kuhn’s structured splitting and completion maps (Kuhn 2006) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Kuhn2006](#ref-Kuhn2006)]; we prove the required finite weight projections and their suspension compatibility directly. For the final passage from leading weight to Adams filtration, we use Kuhn’s lifting theorem (Kuhn 2018) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Kuhn2018](#ref-Kuhn2018)] and the derived ideal-power constructions of (Kuhn and Pereira 2017) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[KuhnPereira2017](#ref-KuhnPereira2017)].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Outline of the proof.

The proof is organized around the last nonzero homology suspension of a spherical class. For a representative $f_0:S^d\to Q_0S^0$, let $f_j:S^{d+j}\to QS^j$ be its successive adjoints and put $z_j=h(f_j)$. Suspension gives $z_{j+1}=\sigma_*z_j$, and the homology degree bounds imply that $z_j=0$ for $j>d$. Thus a nonzero initial image has a last nonzero term $z_e$. We show that this terminal class is a square $z_e=w^2$ whose primitive, Steenrod-annihilated root still has nonzero suspension. At a positive terminal level, the preceding spherical suspension forces the root to have odd degree. At level zero, we instead exclude fourth powers using spectrum pairings and integral characteristic classes (Section <a href="#sec:powers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:powers">4</a>). The mapping cone of the next adjoint then converts the square into a nonzero Steenrod-module extension (Section <a href="#sec:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cones">3</a>).

At positive level $n$, weight counts the inputs of an extended power. The primitives of weight $2^k$, $k\ge1$, are dual to $M_k(n)=\Sigma^n d_0^nD_k$, where $D_k$ is a binary Dickson algebra and $d_0$ is its generator of degree $2^k-1$; the bottom summand is $M_0(n)=\Sigma^n\mathbb F_2$. Section <a href="#sec:weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weights">2</a> constructs these modules, identifies the dual of homology suspension with inclusion of the next $d_0$-ideal, and supplies compatible finite weight projections at level zero. Section <a href="#sec:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algebra">5</a> shows that the cone extension on a nonbottom weight, when its detecting functional lifts from the preceding suspension level, is possible only for $n=k=1$ and in a power-of-two degree. Cartier halving and an injection on even-degree Tor cycles retain the exponent-floor information needed for this restriction.

Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">6</a> assembles the argument. At a positive terminal level the obstruction leaves only the bottom weight, giving stable Hopf detection. At terminal level zero it forces the root to have leading weight two, so the original spherical class has leading weight four. Kuhn’s theorem bounds its Adams filtration by two, and Browder’s identification gives Kervaire detection. Additivity of the detecting invariants then gives the asserted span.

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-route" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-route">1</a> summarizes the two terminal cases.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Conventions.

Spaces are based and of CW homotopy type; for a based space $X$, write $QX=\Omega^\infty\Sigma^\infty X$. Homology and cohomology have coefficients in $\mathbb F_2$ unless stated otherwise. Let $A$ be the mod-2 Steenrod algebra and $I=A^{>0}$ its augmentation ideal. The dual homology action is denoted $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}_*^i$. A homology class is $A$-annihilated if every $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}_*^i$, $i>0$, kills it. Equivalently, its functional on cohomology vanishes on the image of $I$. For a positive graded cohomology algebra $B$, write $Q B=B/B^2$ for its indecomposables.

Primitivity refers to the ordinary space diagonal, with the basepoint as coaugmentation, unless the linear coproduct is explicitly specified. Products in homology of an infinite loop space are its additive Pontryagin products, written $*$ when a distinction is needed. The block pairing introduced later is written $\circ$. Homology suspension between infinite loop levels is $\sigma_*$. We distinguish it from stable homology evaluation $H_*(\Omega^\infty E)\to H_*(E)$, induced by the counit.

<div class="samepage">

The symbol $S$ denotes the sphere spectrum. We write $D_rT=(T^{\wedge r})_{h\Sigma_r}$ for an extended power and $\mathbb P(T)=\bigvee_{r\ge0}D_rT$ for the free commutative ring spectrum on $T$. All constructions are derived and homotopy coherent, including free algebras, pushouts, group completion, localization and parametrized families of maps. We use the usual mod-2 Dyer–Lashof operations on infinite loop spaces and commutative ring spectra (Cohen, Lada, and May 1976; Bruner et al. 1986) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CLM1976](#ref-CLM1976), [BMMS1986](#ref-BMMS1986)]. Whenever a module is written with a suspension shift, polynomial exponents refer to the unshifted monomial; its total degree also includes that shift.

</div>

<a id="sec:weights"></a>
### Weights, primitives, and homology suspension

Homology suspension supplies two inputs to the last nonzero-suspension argument. Its kernel identifies a terminal spherical class as a Pontryagin square. On primitive weight components, its dual identifies a detecting functional with the restriction of a functional at the preceding level. We establish these statements together with finite weight projections at level zero, where group completion requires a filtration in place of the positive-level splitting.

For a spectrum $T$, put $$D_rT=(T^{\wedge r})_{h\Sigma_r},\qquad
 \mathbb P(T)=\bigvee_{r\geq0}D_rT,\qquad D_0T=S.$$ Thus $\mathbb P(T)$ is the free commutative ring spectrum on $T$, and its $r$th summand has *weight* $r$. Throughout, extended powers, free algebras, pushouts, and localizations are derived constructions. We work with their natural maps and families in the relevant homotopy coherent categories. In particular, naturality of a construction includes its action on mapping spaces, not just on homotopy classes of individual maps.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### A structured free-algebra model

Let $C(X)$ be the reduced free $E_\infty$ monoid on a based space $X$: the basepoint of $X$ is identified with the monoid unit. The usual approximation and homology theorems identify its group completion with $QX$ and calculate its homology by Dyer–Lashof operations (Cohen, Lada, and May 1976, vol. 533, pt. I, Theorems 4.1–4.2 and Corollary 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[CLM1976](#ref-CLM1976)]. We first record the structured splitting that will specify our weights, including their behavior in families.

<a id="stmt-prop-free-ring"></a>
<div id="prop:free-ring" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4**. *There is a natural equivalence of augmented commutative ring spectra $$\Sigma^\infty_+ C(X)\simeq\mathbb P(\Sigma^\infty X).$$ The reduced suspension spectrum of $C(X)$, based at its unit, corresponds to the sum of the positive weights. The adjoint of projection to weight one is the canonical map $$C(X)\longrightarrow QX.$$ This map is group completion and is an equivalence when $X$ is connected. In the connected case, the weight-one projection is consequently the stable counit $\Sigma^\infty QX\to\Sigma^\infty X$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-free-ring"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-free-ring</summary>

*Proof.* Let $F$ denote the free unbased commutative monoid in spaces. The reduced free monoid is the homotopy pushout $$C(X)\simeq F(X)\amalg_{F(*)}*.$$ Indeed, mapping this pushout into a commutative monoid $Y$ gives the homotopy fibre of $\operatorname{Map}(X,Y)\to\operatorname{Map}(*,Y)=Y$ over the unit. This is exactly the mapping space of based maps from $X$ to $Y$, based at its unit. The pushout therefore has the required reduced free universal property, including the basepoint homotopy.

The functor $\Sigma^\infty_+$ is a strong symmetric monoidal left adjoint from cartesian spaces to spectra. On commutative algebra objects it is again a left adjoint, and hence preserves this pushout. Write $T=\Sigma^\infty X$. The basepoint section and augmentation give a natural stable splitting $$\Sigma^\infty_+X\simeq S\vee T.$$ Under it, the spectrum map induced by the basepoint is the inclusion of the first summand. Since the free commutative algebra functor sends a direct sum to a coproduct of algebras, the pushout after suspension is $$\begin{aligned}
 \Sigma^\infty_+C(X)
 &\simeq\mathbb P(S\vee T)\amalg_{\mathbb P(S)}S\\
 &\simeq
 \bigl(\mathbb P(S)\amalg\mathbb P(T)\bigr)
       \amalg_{\mathbb P(S)}S
 \simeq\mathbb P(T).
 \end{aligned}$$ Here $\mathbb P(S)\to S$ sends its generator to the ring unit. The augmentation of the resulting algebra sends $T$ to zero. Thus the positive-weight summands are precisely the augmentation fibre, or equivalently the reduced suspension spectrum at the unit. In this description the generating spectrum map is the *difference* between the inclusion of $X$ and its basepoint section.

We identify the weight-one adjoint using the algebra projection $$\mathbb P(T)\longrightarrow S\oplus T,$$ where the target is the split square-zero algebra. For any spectrum $M$, the fibre over the unit point of $$\mathrm{GL}_1(S\oplus M)\longrightarrow\mathrm{GL}_1S$$ identifies with $\Omega^\infty M$, via $y\mapsto1+y$, as a grouplike commutative monoid. To see the structure as well as the underlying space, expand a multiproduct using distributivity in $(S\oplus M)^{\wedge t}$. The term with no $M$ factors gives the base unit. The terms with one $M$ factor give the sum of the inputs. Every term with two or more $M$ factors vanishes by the square-zero structure. These formulas respect permutations and iterated products: the structure maps on the fibre are the additive sum-over-fibres maps. They therefore give the stated identification with the additive infinite loop space of $M$.

The adjunction from the suspension ring now gives an $E_\infty$ map $C(X)\to\Omega^\infty T$. On the generating based space it is the usual map $X\to\Omega^\infty\Sigma^\infty X$. Reduced freeness identifies it with the canonical stabilization map. Equivalently, maps from its group completion into a grouplike commutative monoid $\Omega^\infty E$ are based maps $X\to\Omega^\infty E$, or spectrum maps $\Sigma^\infty X\to E$. This identifies the group completion with $QX$. For connected $X$, the reduced free monoid has trivial $\pi_0$, so it is already grouplike and no group completion is needed. Under $C(X)\simeq QX$, the adjoint of the weight-one projection is then the identity of $QX$. By the suspension-spectrum adjunction this says exactly that the projection is the stable counit. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 5*. For connected $X$, the underlying stable decomposition is the Snaith splitting (Snaith 1974, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Snaith1974](#ref-Snaith1974)]. The argument above also establishes the augmented commutative ring-spectrum structure and based naturality needed for multiplication and suspension below. For a free-algebra formulation of this structured splitting, see (Kuhn 2006, Theorem 2.2 and Appendix A) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Kuhn2006](#ref-Kuhn2006)].

</div>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Suspension and the linear coproduct

<a id="stmt-lem-weight-suspension"></a>
<div id="lem:weight-suspension" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:weight-suspension"></a>
**Lemma 6**. *Under the splitting of Proposition <a href="#prop:free-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:free-ring">4</a>, the stable adjoint suspension on reduced pieces is weight preserving. At weight $r$ it is the natural map $$\label{eq:weight-suspension}
 \Sigma D_rT\longrightarrow D_r(\Sigma T),
 \qquad T=\Sigma^\infty X,$$ formed by using the same suspension coordinate in all $r$ factors. For $T=\Sigma^nS$, this is the Thom spectrum map induced by the inclusion of the diagonal trivial line into the permutation representation.*

*Under group completion the map is the ordinary homology suspension $\sigma_*:H_*QX\to H_{*+1}Q\Sigma X$. It commutes with the upper-index Dyer–Lashof operations on reduced classes and kills products of two positive-dimensional classes. If $c$ is the class of a component point and $z$ has positive degree, then $$\sigma_*(c*z)=\sigma_*z.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-weight-suspension"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weight-suspension</summary>

*Proof.* For $v\in S^1$, the based map $x\mapsto v\wedge x$ induces $C(X)\to C(\Sigma X)$. These maps form a pointed family, since the basepoint of $S^1$ induces the constant map to the monoid unit. Its adjoint is $$S^1\wedge C(X)\longrightarrow C(\Sigma X).$$ Since $\Sigma X$ is connected, the target is $Q\Sigma X$. On the generating space $X$, adjunction identifies this family with the usual stabilization into $\Omega Q\Sigma X\simeq QX$. Reduced freeness and group completion therefore identify its adjoint with the usual structure map of the infinite loop spaces.

The positive-weight equivalence in Proposition <a href="#prop:free-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:free-ring">4</a> is a natural equivalence between the reduced functors $$X\longmapsto\operatorname{fib}(\Sigma^\infty_+C(X)\to S),
 \qquad
 X\longmapsto\bigvee_{r>0}D_r(\Sigma^\infty X).$$ Both send the one-point based space to zero. Their action on mapping spaces therefore respects the basepoint given by factorization through that space, including its nullhomotopy. Apply this naturality to the pointed mapping-space family $v\mapsto(v\wedge-)$, and then take the stable adjunction. On the $r$th extended power the same $v$ is used in every tensor factor. This gives <a href="#eq:weight-suspension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-suspension">[eq:weight-suspension]</a> with no mixing of weights. For a sphere, $D_r(\Sigma^nS)$ is the Thom spectrum of $n\rho_r$ over $B\Sigma_r$, where $\rho_r$ is the permutation representation. The map in question is the Thom map associated to $$n\rho_r\oplus\mathbb R\longrightarrow(n+1)\rho_r,$$ using the diagonal line $\mathbb R\subset\rho_r$.

The commutation rule with upper-index Dyer–Lashof operations is the usual suspension rule for free infinite loop space homology (May 1977, sec. 20, p. 481) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[May1977](#ref-May1977)]. The product rules can also be read directly from the adjunction of loop multiplication: the suspension of loop concatenation factors through the pinch map and gives the sum of the two suspensions. Thus a cross product of two positive-dimensional classes has zero image. For a product with a component point, the surviving term is the suspension of the positive-dimensional factor, multiplied by the augmentation of the point class, which is one. ◻

</details>

For every $n\geq0$ we may equip $\mathbb P(\Sigma^nS)$ with the *linear coproduct*: it is the commutative ring map whose restriction to the generating spectrum is the sum of its inclusions in the two factors. At $n=0$ this is auxiliary structure and is not asserted to be the space diagonal. At positive levels it is the space diagonal, as the following observation shows.

<a id="stmt-lem-linear-diagonal"></a>
<div id="lem:linear-diagonal" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7**. *For a suspension $X$, the space diagonal on $C(X)$ corresponds under Proposition <a href="#prop:free-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:free-ring">4</a> to the linear coproduct on $\mathbb P(\Sigma^\infty X)$. Its component from weight $r$ to weights $(i,r-i)$ is the stable transfer for $$\Sigma_i\times\Sigma_{r-i}\subset\Sigma_r.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-linear-diagonal"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-linear-diagonal</summary>

*Proof.* In terms of the reduced generator, the diagonal is the sum of its two linear inclusions and the term induced by the reduced diagonal $X\to X\wedge X$. The latter is null for a suspension. Because the source ring is free, a homotopy on the generating spectrum gives the required homotopy of commutative ring maps.

Apply $D_r$ to the linear map $T\to T\vee T$. The terms choosing the first summand $i$ times are indexed by the transitive $\Sigma_r$-set $\Sigma_r/(\Sigma_i\times\Sigma_{r-i})$. Equivariantly, the map into these terms is the diagonal into a finite sum indexed by that set. On homotopy orbits it is the stable transfer to the indicated subgroup. This describes every component, including the two end components. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The Steenrod modules in a primitive weight

At weight $r>0$, let $P_r(n)$ denote the subspace of $H_*D_r(\Sigma^nS)$ primitive for the linear coproduct: all components with $0<i<r$ vanish. Its degreewise dual is the quotient of $H^*D_r(\Sigma^nS)$ by the sum of the images of the proper two-block cohomology transfers. At each fixed weight these groups are of finite type, so this duality is exact degree by degree. For $n>0$, put $$B_n=\widetilde H^*(QS^n),\qquad QB_n=B_n/B_n^2.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:linear-diagonal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:linear-diagonal">7</a> identifies the transfer quotients with the corresponding weight parts of $QB_n$.

At weight $2^k$, restriction to the regular elementary abelian subgroup of $\Sigma_{2^k}$ will identify this quotient with an ideal in a polynomial invariant ring. We first describe that ring.

<a id="eq:dickson-polynomial"></a>
For $k\geq1$, write $$P_k=\mathbb F_2[x_1,\ldots,x_k],\qquad |x_i|=1,
 \qquad D_k=P_k^{\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_k(\mathbb F_2)}
       =\mathbb F_2[d_0,\ldots,d_{k-1}],$$ where $|d_j|=2^k-2^j$. Here $D_k$ is a Dickson algebra, in contrast to the extended-power spectrum $D_rT$. We suppress the rank $k$ on the generators $d_j$ and set $d_k=1$. The Dickson generators are the coefficients of the additive polynomial $$\label{eq:dickson-polynomial}
 \prod_{a\in\operatorname{span}\{x_1,\ldots,x_k\}}(U+a)
   =\sum_{j=0}^k d_jU^{2^j}.$$ The invariant-ring description is Dickson’s theorem (Dickson 1911) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Dickson1911](#ref-Dickson1911)]. We recall the root-polynomial proof of the binary case, also developed in Wilkerson’s account (Wilkerson 1983, sec. I) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Wilkerson1983](#ref-Wilkerson1983)].

<a id="stmt-lem-dickson-ring"></a>
<div id="lem:dickson-ring" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8**. *The coefficients $d_0,\ldots,d_{k-1}$ in <a href="#eq:dickson-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dickson-polynomial">[eq:dickson-polynomial]</a> are algebraically independent, have degrees $2^k-2^j$, and generate $P_k^{\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_k(\mathbb F_2)}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-dickson-ring"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-dickson-ring</summary>

*Proof.* The product over a finite binary vector space is additive in $U$. Indeed, if $W'=W\oplus\langle a\rangle$ and its root polynomial for $W$ is $P_W$, then $$P_{W'}(U)=P_W(U)P_W(U+a)
          =P_W(U)^2+P_W(a)P_W(U).$$ Induction from $P_{\{0\}}(U)=U$ proves additivity and the displayed powers of $U$. Homogeneity gives the degrees of the coefficients. They are invariant because an invertible binary linear change of variables permutes the roots.

Put $B=\mathbb F_2[d_0,\ldots,d_{k-1}]\subset P_k$ and $L=\mathbb F_2(x_1,\ldots,x_k)$. Each $x_i$ satisfies the monic root polynomial, so $P_k$ is integral over $B$. Therefore $B$ has transcendence degree $k$; its $k$ indicated generators are algebraically independent and $B$ is integrally closed. The derivative of the root polynomial is the nonzero product $d_0$ of all nonzero binary linear forms. Its splitting field over $\operatorname{Frac}B$ is $L$. Every automorphism of this splitting field acts invertibly and linearly on the root space; conversely every such linear transformation fixes the coefficients. Consequently $L^{\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_k(\mathbb F_2)}=\operatorname{Frac}B$. An invariant polynomial lies in this fraction field and is integral over $B$, hence belongs to $B$. This proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-weight-modules"></a>
<div id="lem:weight-modules" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9**. *The only nonzero positive-weight transfer quotients are at weights $r=2^k$, $k\geq0$. As Steenrod modules they are <a id="eq:weight-modules"></a>$$\label{eq:weight-modules}
 M_0(n)=\Sigma^n\mathbb F_2,\qquad
 M_k(n)=\Sigma^n d_0^nD_k\quad(k\geq1),\qquad n\geq0.$$ For $n>0$ these give the weight decomposition of $QB_n$. The action on $d_0^nD_k$ is its ordinary action as an ideal in $P_k$.*

<a id="eq:module-suspension"></a>
*The dual of <a href="#eq:weight-suspension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-suspension">[eq:weight-suspension]</a> on these quotients is, after the common grading suspension, the inclusion $$\label{eq:module-suspension}
 d_0^{n+1}D_k\hookrightarrow d_0^nD_k
 \qquad(k\geq1).$$ In weight one it is the identity with the corresponding shift.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-weight-modules"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weight-modules</summary>

<a id="eq:free-homology-basis"></a>
