All Laurent polynomials and sums used here are finite, so this deletion requires no completion.

We have proved that the representative is a boundary already over $E_{[k]}$ with coefficients in $M$. Equivalently, it is zero in $I_{E_{[k]}}\otimes_{E_{[k]}}M$, and hence also in $I_E\otimes_EM$. Its image in $I\otimes_AM$ is zero, which proves the lemma. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
#### Proof of the obstruction lemma

<a id="stmt-lem-algebra-odd-tensor"></a>
<div id="lem:algebra-odd-tensor" class="lemma">

**Lemma 27**. *If $n>0$, $m\geq3$ is odd, and $v\in M_k(n)$ is homogeneous of degree $m$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v=0$ in $I\otimes_AM_k(n)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-algebra-odd-tensor"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-algebra-odd-tensor</summary>

*Proof.* The Adem relation $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m=\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{m-1}$ gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v=\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{m-1}v$. For $n\geq2$ the second operation exceeds the internal polynomial degree $m-n$, so this is zero. For $n=1$ it is the internal square $v^2$. By linearity we may take $v=d_0^c\prod_{j\geq1}d_j^{b_j}$, where $c\geq1$. For $k\geq2$, formula <a href="#eq:milnor-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:milnor-action">[eq:milnor-action]</a> gives $$v^2=\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1\left(
 d_0^{2c-1}d_1^{2b_1+1}\prod_{j=2}^{k-1}d_j^{2b_j}
 \right).$$ The preimage belongs to $d_0D_k$. For $k=1$ use instead $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1(d_0^{2c-1})=d_0^{2c}$. Balancing once more and using $(\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1)^2=0$ proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

The even-degree argument below uses the following square formulas only in its final case. These are the square-action formulas of Hai and Hung, recorded in (Hung 1991, Theorem B) <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[Hung1991](#ref-Hung1991)]; the proof below uses the root-polynomial method of (Wilkerson 1983, sec. II) <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[Wilkerson1983](#ref-Wilkerson1983)].

<a id="stmt-lem-algebra-near-top-square"></a>
<div id="lem:algebra-near-top-square" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:algebra-total-dickson-square"></a>
<a id="eq:algebra-one-below-top"></a>
**Lemma 28**. *The total Steenrod square on a Dickson generator is $$\label{eq:algebra-total-dickson-square}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(d_j)=d_j^2+
       \left(\sum_{i=0}^jd_i\right)
       \left(\sum_{a=j+1}^kd_a\right).$$ In particular the operation one below the top on $d_j$ is $d_0d_{j+1}$. For a positive-degree monomial $v=\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}d_j^{b_j}$ of internal degree $d$, $$\label{eq:algebra-one-below-top}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{d-1}v
 =v^2\sum_{j:\ b_j\ \text{odd}}\frac{d_0d_{j+1}}{d_j^2}.$$ Every displayed summand is a polynomial.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-algebra-near-top-square"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-algebra-near-top-square</summary>

*Proof.* Apply the total square simultaneously to the variables $x_s$ and $U$ in <a href="#eq:algebra-dickson-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra-dickson-polynomial">[eq:algebra-dickson-polynomial]</a>. Since $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(U+a)=(U+a)(U+a+1)$ and $P$ is additive, $$\sum_{j=0}^k\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(d_j)(U+U^2)^{2^j}
 =P(U)P(U+1)=P(U)^2+P(1)P(U).$$ Comparison of the coefficients of $U^{2^j}$ gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(d_0)=P(1)d_0$ and, for $1\leq j<k$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(d_j)+\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(d_{j-1})=d_{j-1}^2+P(1)d_j.$$ Summing these identities yields $$\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}(d_j)=P(1)\sum_{i=0}^jd_i+\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}d_i^2,$$ which is <a href="#eq:algebra-total-dickson-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra-total-dickson-square">[eq:algebra-total-dickson-square]</a>. The term of degree $2|d_j|-1$ in that formula is exactly $d_0d_{j+1}$. In the Cartan expansion of an operation one below the top on a product, exactly one factor receives its operation one below the top and every other factor receives its top operation. Equal choices cancel in pairs, leaving precisely <a href="#eq:algebra-one-below-top" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra-one-below-top">[eq:algebra-one-below-top]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing"></a>
<div id="lem:algebra-even-vanishing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 29**. *Let $m$ be even and not a power of two, let $b=2^{\nu_2(m)}$, and let $v\in M_k(n)$ be a degree-$m$ monomial with $n>0$ and $d_0$-exponent $c$. If <a href="#eq:halving-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:halving-bounds">[eq:halving-bounds]</a> holds, then $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v=0$ in $I\otimes_AM_k(n)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing</summary>

*Proof.* The tensor is a cycle, because $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m$ exceeds the internal degree $m-n$ of $v$. We induct on $\nu_2(m)$, using Lemma <a href="#lem:tor-injection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:tor-injection">26</a> throughout; its parity assumption follows from <a href="#eq:halving-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:halving-bounds">[eq:halving-bounds]</a>. Here we halve coinvariants of the current ideal, with floor $n$. This differs from the floor $n-1$ used for the functional in Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-functional-halving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-functional-halving">25</a>.

Suppose first that $b\geq4$. The image of $v$ in exterior coinvariants is either zero, in which case the injectivity lemma gives the result immediately, or is an above-floor survivor. Indeed $c\geq n+b-2\geq n+2$. In this case the first allowed exponent in the above-floor part of $M_k(n)$ is $n+2$, and its Cartier image has first exponent $$n'=\frac{n+k}{2}.$$ With this shift the image is $M_k(n')$, with total degrees halved. The image of our cycle in <a href="#eq:tor-injection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tor-injection">[eq:tor-injection]</a> consequently comes from $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{m/2}\otimes v'$ in $I\otimes_AM_k(n')$, where $v'=C(v)$. Put $c'=(c+k-2)/2$ and $b'=b/2$. Then $$n'\equiv k\pmod{b'},\qquad
 c'-n'+2=\frac{c-n+2}{2}\geq b'.$$ Thus the same bounds hold for $m/2,n',c'$. Since $m/2$ is still not a power of two, induction makes this latter tensor zero, and injectivity gives the required vanishing of the original cycle.

It remains to treat $b=2$. Write $m=2L$, with $L\geq3$ odd. All coinvariants of $M_k(n)$ have even total degree: this is clear at the floor from <a href="#eq:algebra-degree-parity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra-degree-parity">[eq:algebra-degree-parity]</a>, and above it from $c\equiv k\equiv n\pmod2$. Regrade them by half and call the resulting $A$-module $C_n$. The projected tensor is $$\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^L\otimes[v]
   =\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{L-1}[v].$$ The second factor is the class of the original operation $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{m-2}v$. It suffices to show that this class is zero or an image of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1$ in $C_n$, since balancing then introduces $(\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1)^2=0$.

If $n\geq3$, the operation already exceeds the internal degree of $v$. If $n=2$, then $k$ is even and at least two, and the operation is the internal square $v^2$. This lies above the floor and has all nonzero-index exponents even, so it is zero in coinvariants by Lemma <a href="#lem:milnor-coinvariants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:milnor-coinvariants">22</a>.

Suppose $n=1$, so $k$ is odd. For $k=1$ the coinvariant basis, including the floor, consists of all odd monomials in $d_0$. The map $C_x$ identifies $C_1$ with $\Sigma D_1$, and the image of $v=d_0^{2L-1}$ is $d_0^{L-1}$. Its image under $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{L-1}$ is $$d_0^{2L-2}=\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1(d_0^{2L-3}),$$ as required.

Finally, suppose $n=1$ and $k\geq3$ is odd. The internal degree of $v$ is $m-1$, so Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-near-top-square" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-near-top-square">28</a> applies to $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{m-2}v$. In each summand of <a href="#eq:algebra-one-below-top" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra-one-below-top">[eq:algebra-one-below-top]</a>, at most one exponent of $d_j$ with $j\geq1$ is odd. Since there are at least two such indices, every term above the floor dies in exterior coinvariants. The only possible floor term comes from the index $j=0$ when $v=d_0w$, where $w$ involves only $d_j$, $j\geq1$. It is $d_0d_1w^2$.

This remaining term is an image for the halved first square. Formula <a href="#eq:algebra-total-dickson-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebra-total-dickson-square">[eq:algebra-total-dickson-square]</a> gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^2d_2=d_1$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1d_0=\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^2d_0=0$ for $k\geq3$. Together with <a href="#eq:milnor-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:milnor-action">[eq:milnor-action]</a> and the Cartan formula, it therefore gives the explicit identity <a id="eq:algebra-floor-witness"></a>$$\label{eq:algebra-floor-witness}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^2(d_0d_2w^2)
 =d_0d_1w^2+
   d_0^3d_2\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial d_1}\right)^2.$$ The extra term is an exterior hit in $d_0D_k$, with preimage $$q_0\left(
 d_0^2d_1d_2\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial d_1}\right)^2
 \right)
 =d_0^3d_2\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial d_1}\right)^2.$$ Hence the class of $d_0d_1w^2$ is the original $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^2$-image of $[d_0d_2w^2]$, or equivalently a $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1$-image after halving. This completes the final case, and the injectivity lemma finishes the proof. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-algebra-even-vanishing</summary>

*Proof of Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-obstruction">20</a>.* The odd assertion is Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-odd-tensor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-odd-tensor">27</a>. For even $m$, the functional hypothesis gives <a href="#eq:halving-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:halving-bounds">[eq:halving-bounds]</a> by Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-functional-halving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-functional-halving">25</a>. If $m$ is not a power of two, Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-even-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-even-vanishing">29</a> contradicts <a href="#eq:obstruction-tensor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:obstruction-tensor">[eq:obstruction-tensor]</a>. If $m$ is a power of two, the last assertion of Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-functional-halving" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-functional-halving">25</a> gives $n=k=1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:conclusion"></a>
### The last nonzero suspension and the sphere

Let $\alpha\in\pi_d^S$, $d>0$, have nonzero mod-2 Hurewicz image. Represent it by $f_0:S^d\to Q_0S^0$, and write $$f_j:S^{d+j}\longrightarrow QS^j,\qquad
z_j=h(f_j)=\sigma_*^j h(f_0)$$ for the successive adjoints and their Hurewicz images. All the $z_j$ are primitive and $A$-annihilated. There is a largest $e\ge0$ with $z_e\ne0$. Indeed, when $j>d$, the degree $d+j$ lies strictly between $j$ and $2j$; the free infinite-loop homology description has no class in that interval.

<a id="stmt-lem-last-root"></a>
<div id="lem:last-root" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:last-square"></a>
**Lemma 30**. *For some $m\ge2$, $$\label{eq:last-square}
z_e=w^2,\qquad |w|=m-1,\qquad d+e=2m-2,$$ where $w$ is primitive and $A$-annihilated, and $\sigma_*w\ne0$. If $e\ge1$, then $|w|$ is odd.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-last-root"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-last-root</summary>

*Proof.* Since $\sigma_*z_e=0$, Lemmas <a href="#lem:suspension-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:suspension-kernel">11</a> and <a href="#lem:primitive-roots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:primitive-roots">12</a> give <a href="#eq:last-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:last-square">[eq:last-square]</a> with a primitive $A$-annihilated root. Its degree is positive, so $m\ge2$.

If $e=0$ and $\sigma_*w=0$, the same lemmas make $w$ a square. Then $z_0$ is a fourth power, contradicting Proposition <a href="#prop:no-fourth-powers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:no-fourth-powers">16</a>. Thus the root suspends nontrivially in this case.

<a id="eq:root-parity"></a>
For $e\ge1$, we instead use the preceding spherical class $z_{e-1}$ to prove that $|w|$ is odd. First suppose that $e\ge2$, so both levels have the direct weight decomposition of Lemma <a href="#lem:weight-modules" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weight-modules">9</a>. Choose a nonzero weight $2^l$ component of $z_e$. Here $l\ge1$, since the degree $d+e$ is greater than the bottom degree $e$. Its functional on $M_l(e)$ comes, after the grading shift, from the functional of $z_{e-1}$ on $M_l(e-1)$. Vanishing of $z_{e+1}$ says that it annihilates $d_0^{e+1}D_l$, by <a href="#eq:module-suspension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:module-suspension">[eq:module-suspension]</a>. It must therefore pair nontrivially with some floor monomial $$d_0^e\prod_{j=1}^{l-1}d_j^{b_j}.$$ In $M_l(e-1)$ this monomial is above the floor. Since the lifted functional is $A$-annihilated, Lemma <a href="#lem:milnor-coinvariants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:milnor-coinvariants">22</a> gives $$e\equiv l\pmod2,\qquad b_j\text{ odd for every }j\ge1.$$ The degree formula and <a href="#eq:last-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:last-square">[eq:last-square]</a> yield $$\label{eq:root-parity}
m-1=2^{l-1}e+
\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}(2^{l-1}-2^{j-1})b_j.$$ For $l\ge2$, only the term with $j=1$ is odd. For $l=1$, the right side is $e$, which is odd by the congruence. Thus $m-1$ is odd.

If $e=1$, <a href="#eq:last-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:last-square">[eq:last-square]</a> shows that $d$ is odd. Choose the leading weight $2^l$ of $z_0$. Lemma <a href="#lem:zero-weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:zero-weights">10</a> says that it survives to $z_1$, with a functional lifted from the annihilated functional on $M_l(0)$. Since $z_2=0$, the same floor argument applies to this component of $z_1$, and again <a href="#eq:root-parity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:root-parity">[eq:root-parity]</a> proves that $m-1$ is odd.

An odd-degree class cannot be a square. Therefore the suspension-kernel and primitive-root lemmas imply $\sigma_*w\ne0$ whenever $e\ge1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:last-functional"></a>
Set $n=e+1$. The map $$f_n:S^{2m-1}\longrightarrow T=QS^n$$ has zero Hurewicz image, and its previous adjoint has image $w^2$. The loop product under $QS^{n-1}\simeq\Omega QS^n$ agrees with the additive Pontryagin product. Thus Section <a href="#sec:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cones">3</a> applies with $x=w$. The nonzero functional $$\label{eq:last-functional}
\lambda=\langle -,\sigma_*w\rangle
\quad\text{on }\bigl(Q\widetilde H^*(QS^n)\bigr)^m$$ gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m\otimes v\ne0$ for every monomial $v$ on which it has value one.

We first deduce that $m$ is even. If $m$ were odd, then $m\ge3$. Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-obstruction">20</a> excludes a nonzero pairing on every $M_k(n)$ with $k\ge1$. A bottom pairing requires $m=n$; on $M_0(n)=\Sigma^n\mathbb F_2$, the balanced tensor product is the shift of $I/I^2$. But $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^m=\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^1\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{m-1}\in I^2$, contradicting Corollary <a href="#cor:tensor-detection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:tensor-detection">15</a>. Since $\lambda\ne0$, this rules out odd $m$.

<a id="stmt-prop-hopf-case"></a>
<div id="prop:hopf-case" class="proposition">

**Proposition 31** (The positive terminal stage). *If $e\ge1$, then $\alpha$ has nonzero mod-2 stable Hopf invariant and $d\in\{1,3,7\}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-hopf-case"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-hopf-case</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose that $\sigma_*w$ has a nonzero component of weight $2^k$ with $k\ge1$. Its degree-$m$ functional on $M_k(n)$ is lifted from the $A$-annihilated functional of $w$ on $M_k(e)$. The nonzero tensor supplied by <a href="#eq:last-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:last-functional">[eq:last-functional]</a> therefore satisfies all the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-obstruction">20</a>. That lemma forces $n=1$, contrary to $n=e+1\ge2$. Hence $\lambda$ is nonzero on the bottom summand, giving $$m=n=e+1,\qquad d=e.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:cone-square" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cone-square">13</a> now says that the extension of the weight-one bottom class in $C_{f_n}$ has nonzero $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^n$, equal to the top relative class.

Let $\epsilon:\Sigma^\infty QS^n\to\Sigma^nS$ be the stable counit and define $$\alpha_n=\epsilon\circ\Sigma^\infty f_n:
\Sigma^{2n-1}S\longrightarrow\Sigma^nS.$$ This is the stable adjoint representing $\alpha$ in stem $d=n-1$. The counit is the weight-one projection, by Proposition <a href="#prop:free-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:free-ring">4</a>. The commutative square with identity on the attaching sphere induces a map $$\Sigma^\infty C_{f_n}\longrightarrow C_{\alpha_n}$$ to the stable two-cell cofiber. Its pullback carries the bottom class to the weight-one bottom class and the top relative class to the top relative class. Both top relative classes inject in mod-2 cohomology. Naturality therefore makes $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^n$ nonzero in $C_{\alpha_n}$. This is nonzero mod-2 stable Hopf detection of $\alpha$.

Adams’s Hopf invariant one theorem, in its stable two-cell form (Adams 1960, Theorem 1.1.1(d)) <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[Adams1960](#ref-Adams1960)], permits $n=1,2,4,8$. Since $d=n-1>0$, we obtain $d=1,3,7$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-kervaire-case"></a>
<div id="prop:kervaire-case" class="proposition">

**Proposition 32** (The terminal stage zero). *If $e=0$, then for some $j\ge1$, $$d=2^{j+1}-2,$$ and $\alpha$ has Kervaire invariant one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-kervaire-case"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-kervaire-case</summary>

*Proof.* *Leading weight.* Here $n=1$, and we have proved that $m$ is even. Thus $|w|=m-1$ is odd. Choose the leading weight $2^k$ of $w$ in the level-zero expansion. It has $k\ge1$, because $w$ has positive degree. By Lemma <a href="#lem:zero-weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:zero-weights">10</a>, this weight survives in $\sigma_*w$, with functional lifted from an annihilated functional on $D_k=M_k(0)$. Combining Corollary <a href="#cor:tensor-detection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:tensor-detection">15</a> with Lemma <a href="#lem:algebra-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:algebra-obstruction">20</a> gives $$k=1,\qquad m=2^j\quad\text{for some }j\ge1.$$ Consequently $z_0=w^2$ has leading weight four: its leading term is the square of the nonzero weight-two term, and Frobenius is injective in the associated free polynomial homology algebra. Also $d=2m-2=2^{j+1}-2$.

*Adams filtration.* We bound the Adams filtration of the given element. Let $\mathcal I$ be the augmentation ideal of $\Sigma^\infty_+QS^0$. Kuhn’s lifting theorem (Kuhn 2018, Theorems 1.2–1.3) <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[Kuhn2018](#ref-Kuhn2018)], applied to $X=S$ and the coefficient ring $H\mathbb F_2$, says that an element of mod-2 Adams filtration at least $s_0$ has Hurewicz image lifted from $H\mathbb F_2\wedge\mathcal I^{2^{s_0}}$, where $\mathcal I^{2^{s_0}}$ is the corresponding derived augmentation-ideal power of $\Sigma^\infty_+QS^0$. Its hypotheses hold: both spectra are connective and $\pi_0S\to\pi_0H\mathbb F_2$ is onto. At the prime two the localization away from $(p-1)!$ in that theorem is vacuous. The theorem does not require $QS^0$ to be connected.

To use this with the leading weight, take the augmented algebra map $$\Sigma^\infty_+QS^0\longrightarrow\mathbb P_{\le7}(S\{u\})$$ from <a href="#eq:weight-projections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-projections">[eq:weight-projections]</a>, and let $J$ denote the positive ideal in its target. Regard $J$ as a nonunital algebra in the full category of nonnegatively graded spectra, with ordinary Day convolution and with $J_r=0$ for $r=0$ and $r\ge8$. Using the reduced commutative operad $\mathrm{Com}$, whose arities are positive, its derived eighth power is the bar construction $$J^8=B(\mathrm{Com}_{\ge8},\mathrm{Com},J);$$ see (Kuhn 2018, Definition 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[Kuhn2018](#ref-Kuhn2018)] and (Kuhn and Pereira 2017, Definition 3.1 and Example 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[KuhnPereira2017](#ref-KuhnPereira2017)]. Compute this derived bar construction with a free resolution in strictly positive weights. Every simplicial summand then has at least eight inputs of weight at least one; no nullary operation can remove such an input. Its total weight is therefore at least eight. The canonical map $J^8\to J$ preserves weight and is zero, since $J$ has only weights one through seven. The direct-sum functor from the full graded category is strong symmetric monoidal and preserves realizations and homotopy orbits. It therefore carries the graded free resolution and bar construction to the corresponding ungraded derived construction, with the same zero canonical map. Extension of coefficients to $H\mathbb F_2$ also preserves this construction (Kuhn and Pereira 2017, Theorem 3.4(d)) <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[KuhnPereira2017](#ref-KuhnPereira2017)]; in particular, the canonical map remains zero after smashing with $H\mathbb F_2$.

By naturality, the Hurewicz image of an element of Adams filtration at least three would vanish under every positive-weight projection below eight. The weight-four image just found is nonzero. Thus $\alpha$ has Adams filtration at most two. This uses only a lower bound on possible weights; it does not identify Adams filtration with an exact weight.

*Kervaire detection.* Positive stable stems are finite by Serre’s finiteness theorem (Serre 1951, chap. V, §3, Proposition 3) <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[Serre1951](#ref-Serre1951)]: choose an odd sphere dimension $N>d+1$, so that $\pi_{N+d}(S^N)$ is both stable and finite. Their odd-primary parts have zero mod-2 Hurewicz image and zero mod-2 detecting invariants, so we may discard them and use the convergent mod-2 Adams spectral sequence for the remaining two-primary group. More precisely, Adams’s convergence theorem identifies its successive filtration quotients with $E_\infty$ and its filtration intersection with the prime-to-two torsion, which has been removed (Adams 1958, Theorem 2.1(i),(v),(vi)) <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[Adams1958](#ref-Adams1958)]. Write $F^s\pi_d^S$ for the subgroup of Adams filtration at least $s$ in this two-primary group. Filtration zero has no positive-stem sphere class. Filtration one has internal degrees $2^a$, hence stems $2^a-1$; none is the positive even stem in question. In filtration two, the classical calculation (Adams 1960, Theorem 2.5.1(1)–(2)) <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[Adams1960](#ref-Adams1960)] gives basis elements $h_bh_a$, where $b\ge a\ge0$ and $b\ne a+1$. The identity $2^a+2^b=2^{j+1}$ forces $a=b=j$. Therefore $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^{2,2^{j+1}}(\mathbb F_2,\mathbb F_2)=\mathbb F_2\{h_j^2\},$$ and the given element is detected by the surviving class $h_j^2$.

Browder’s detection theorem identifies the framed Kervaire invariant of an individual stable sphere element with its detection by $h_j^2$. The original framed Arf identification and secondary pairing occur in (Browder 1969, Theorem 3.2, Proposition 7.6 and Theorems 7.7–7.8) <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[Browder1969](#ref-Browder1969)]; the precise elementwise statement is also recorded in (Behrens et al. 2020, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-54"></a>[[BHHM2020](#ref-BHHM2020)]. This statement includes the low framed cases $j=1,2,3$. It applies to the particular $\alpha$ above, whose nonzero class in $F^2\pi_d^S/F^3\pi_d^S$ is represented by the surviving $h_j^2$, and gives Kervaire invariant one.

In particular the detector is unchanged by adding an element of $F^3\pi_d^S$. Such an element cannot be detected by a filtration-two class, so the same elementwise theorem gives it Kervaire invariant zero; additivity of the framed invariant gives the assertion. This use of Browder’s theorem concerns the invariant of each element, in addition to the existence of a permanent cycle. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-kervaire-case-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-kervaire-case</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:curtis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:curtis">1</a>.* Every positive-degree stable class with nonzero Hurewicz image has a last nonzero suspension. Propositions <a href="#prop:hopf-case" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:hopf-case">31</a> and <a href="#prop:kervaire-case" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:kervaire-case">32</a> show that it has the stated nonzero Hopf or Kervaire invariant. These two degree cases are disjoint.

In a Hopf degree, choose the corresponding class among $\eta,\nu,\sigma$. In a Kervaire degree, choose any Kervaire-invariant-one representative $\theta_j$; its existence in this case is already supplied by the class under discussion. Each detecting invariant is additive with values in $\mathbb F_2$. For the stable Hopf invariant, attach two top cells to the same bottom sphere by representatives $\alpha$ and $\beta$. If $u$ is the bottom class and $a_\alpha,a_\beta$ are the relative top classes, naturality on the two one-cell cofibers gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Sq}}^{d+1}u=H(\alpha)a_\alpha+H(\beta)a_\beta$. Pinching the attaching sphere induces a map from the cofiber of $\alpha+\beta$ to this two-attachment cofiber; it pulls both relative classes to the single top class. Naturality therefore gives $H(\alpha+\beta)=H(\alpha)+H(\beta)$. For the Kervaire invariant, disjoint union of framed manifolds takes their quadratic forms to orthogonal sums and adds their Arf invariants. Subtracting the chosen representative therefore gives an invariant-zero class. If this difference had nonzero Hurewicz image, the proved necessity would force its invariant to be one, a contradiction. Thus its Hurewicz image is zero.

Every nonzero image is consequently the image of the chosen Hopf or Kervaire representative in that degree. These representative images themselves belong to the image, so they span it. No existence assertion for an additional $\theta_j$ is used or obtained from this last step. This proves Curtis’s conjecture in all positive degrees. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "$⇓$suspension kernel; the root survives by parity when $e ≥ 1$, or exclusion of fourth powers when $e = 0$ $⇓$the algebra obstruction with the lifted functional Only the bottom weight remains. Stable Hopf detection $d = 1, 3, 7$ The leading weight of $z_{0}$ is four. Adams filtration two Kervaire detection The proof follows the successive adjoints of one stable sphere class. The root $w$ need not itself be spherical: the next adjoint and its cone provide the realization information used by the Steenrod-module obstruction.",
      "figure_id": "fig:proof-route",
      "pdf_page": 4,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:proof-route",
          "exact_quote": "The proof follows the successive adjoints of one stable\nsphere class. The root \\(w\\) need not itself be spherical: the\nnext adjoint and its cone provide the realization information used\nby the Steenrod-module obstruction.",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "Flowchart diagram organizing the proof structure into sequential boxes connected by downward double arrows:\n1. Top box:\n   - \"An actual sphere map $f_0 : S^d \\to Q_0 S^0$, with $h(f_0) \\neq 0$\"\n   - \"$z_j = \\sigma_*^j h(f_0)$, with $e = \\max\\{j : z_j \\neq 0\\}$\"\n2. Downward double arrow ($\\Downarrow$) labeled to the right:\n   - \"suspension kernel; the root survives by\"\n   - \"parity when $e \\ge 1$, or exclusion of fourth powers when $e = 0$\"\n3. Middle box:\n   - \"$z_e = w^2$, with $w$ primitive and Steenrod-annihilated, $\\sigma_* w \\neq 0$\"\n   - \"The cone of $f_{e+1}$ supplies a nonzero square operation.\"\n4. Downward double arrow ($\\Downarrow$) labeled to the right:\n   - \"the algebra obstruction with the lifted functional\"\n5. Two side-by-side outcome boxes branching below:\n   - Left branch box:\n     - \"$e \\ge 1$\"\n     - \"Only the bottom weight remains.\"\n     - \"Stable Hopf detection\"\n     - \"$d = 1, 3, 7$\"\n   - Right branch box:\n     - \"$e = 0$\"\n     - \"The leading weight of $z_0$ is four.\"\n     - \"Adams filtration two\"\n     - \"Kervaire detection\""
    }
  ],
